   255	class StateBar(v.SystemBar, SepalWidget):
   256	
   257	    """Widget to display quick messages on simple inline status bar
   258	
   259	    Args:
   260	       kwargs (optional): any parameter from a v.SystemBar. If set, 'children' will be overwritten.
   261	    """
   262	
   263	    msg = Unicode("").tag(sync=True)
   264	    "str: the displayed message"
   265	
   266	    loading = Bool(False).tag(sync=True)
   267	    "bool: either or not the circular progress is spinning"
   268	
   269	    progress = None
   270	    "widget: The ProgressCircular widget that will be displayed in the statebar"
   271	
   272	    def __init__(self, **kwargs):
   273	
   274	        self.progress = v.ProgressCircular(
   275	            indeterminate=self.loading,
   276	            value=100,
   277	            small=True,
   278	            size=15,
   279	            color="primary",
   280	            class_="mr-2",
   281	        )
   282	
   283	        # set default parameter
   284	        kwargs["children"] = [self.progress, self.msg]
   285	
   286	        # call the constructor
   287	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
   288	
   289	        jslink((self, "loading"), (self.progress, "indeterminate"))
   290	
   291	    @observe("loading")
   292	    def _change_loading(self, change):
   293	        """Change progress wheel state"""
   294	        self.progress.indeterminate = self.loading
   295	
   296	    @observe("msg")
   297	    def _change_msg(self, change):
   298	        """Change state bar message"""
   299	        self.children = [self.progress, self.msg]
   300	
   301	    def add_msg(self, msg, loading=False):
   302	        """Change current status message"""
   303	        self.msg = msg
   304	        self.loading = loading
   305	
   306	        return self

   309	class Banner(v.Snackbar, SepalWidget):
   310	    """
   311	    Custom Snackbar widget to display messages as a banner in module App.
   312	
   313	    Args:
   314	       msg (str, optional): Message to display in application banner. default to nothing
   315	       type\_ (str, optional): Used to display an appropiate banner color. fallback to "info".
   316	       id_ (str, optional): unique banner identificator.
   317	       persistent (bool, optional): Whether to close automatically based on the lenght of message (False) or make it indefinitely open (True). Overridden if timeout duration is set.
   318	       kwargs (optional): any parameter from a v.Alert. If set, 'vertical' and 'top' will be overwritten.
   319	    """
   320	
   321	    btn_close = None
   322	    "v.Btn: the closing btn of the banner"
   323	
   324	    def __init__(self, msg="", type_="info", id_=None, persistent=True, **kwargs):
   325	
   326	        # compute the type and default to "info" if it's not existing
   327	        type_ = set_type(type_)
   328	
   329	        # create the closing btn
   330	        self.btn_close = v.Btn(
   331	            small=True, text=True, children=[ms.widgets.banner.close]
   332	        )
   333	
   334	        # compute timeout based on the persistent and timeout parameter
   335	        computed_timeout = 0 if persistent is True else self.get_timeout(msg)
   336	
   337	        kwargs["color"] = kwargs.pop("color", type_)
   338	        kwargs["transition"] = kwargs.pop("transition", "scroll-x-transition")
   339	        kwargs["attributes"] = {"id": id_}
   340	        kwargs["v_model"] = kwargs.pop("v_model", True)
   341	        kwargs["timeout"] = kwargs.pop("timeout", False) or computed_timeout
   342	        kwargs["top"] = True
   343	        kwargs["vertical"] = True
   344	        kwargs["children"] = [msg] + [self.btn_close]
   345	        kwargs["class_"] = "mb-1"
   346	
   347	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
   348	
   349	        self.btn_close.on_event("click", self.close)
   350	
   351	    def close(self, *args):
   352	        """Close button event to close snackbar alert"""
   353	        self.v_model = False
   354	
   355	        return
   356	
   357	    def get_timeout(self, text):
   358	        """
   359	        Calculate timeout in miliseconds to read the message
   360	
   361	        Args:
   362	            text (str): the text displayed in the banner to adapt the duration of the timeout
   363	
   364	        Returns
   365	            (int): the duration of the timeout in milliseconds
   366	        """
   367	
   368	        wpm = 180  # readable words per minute
   369	        word_length = 5  # standardized number of chars in calculable word
   370	        words = len(text) / word_length
   371	        words_time = ((words / wpm) * 60) * 1000
   372	
   373	        delay = 1500  # milliseconds before user starts reading the notification
   374	        bonus = 1000  # extra time
   375	
   376	        return delay + words_time + bonus
   377	
   378	    def set_btn(self, nb_banner):
   379	        """
   380	        Change the btn display to inform the user on the number of banners in the queue
   381	
   382	        Args:
   383	            nb_banner (int): the number of banners in the queue
   384	        """
   385	        # do not wrap ms.widget.banner. If you do it won't be recognized by the key-checker of the Translator
   386	        if nb_banner == 0:
   387	            txt = ms.widgets.banner.close
   388	        else:
   389	            txt = ms.widgets.banner.next.format(nb_banner)
   390	        self.btn_close.children = [txt]
   391	
   392	        return
   393

n = int(input())

def generate_patterns(n):
    patterns = []
    def backtrack(current, max_num):
        if len(current) == n:
            patterns.append(current.copy())
            return
        for next_num in range(max_num + 2):
            backtrack(current + [next_num], max(max_num, next_num))
    if n == 0:
        return []
    backtrack([0], 0)
    return patterns

patterns = generate_patterns(n)
strings = [''.join(chr(ord('a') + num) for num in p) for p in patterns]
strings.sort()

<think>
Okay, so I have to find the maximum value of T = xy + yz + zx given two constraints: 2xyz = 1 and (x² + 1)(y² + 1)(z² + 1) = 5. Hmm, this looks a bit challenging, but let me see how to approach it.

First, let me write down the given equations:

1. 2xyz = 1 ⇒ xyz = 1/2
2. (x² + 1)(y² + 1)(z² + 1) = 5
3. Maximize T = xy + yz + zx

I need to relate these equations somehow. Maybe I can expand the second equation to express it in terms of x, y, z and their products. Let's try expanding the left side.

The product (x² + 1)(y² + 1)(z² + 1) can be expanded step by step. Let me do that:

First, expand two factors first:
(x² + 1)(y² + 1) = x²y² + x² + y² + 1.

Now multiply this by (z² + 1):
= [x²y² + x² + y² + 1](z² + 1)
= x²y²z² + x²y² + x²z² + x² + y²z² + y² + z² + 1.

So the expanded form is x²y²z² + x²y² + x²z² + x² + y²z² + y² + z² + 1 = 5.

But since we know that xyz = 1/2, let's compute x²y²z². That would be (xyz)² = (1/2)² = 1/4. So substituting that in:

1/4 + x²y² + x²z² + x² + y²z² + y² + z² + 1 = 5.

Combine the constants: 1/4 + 1 = 5/4. So:

5/4 + x²y² + x²z² + x² + y²z² + y² + z² = 5.

Subtract 5/4 from both sides:

x²y² + x²z² + x² + y²z² + y² + z² = 5 - 5/4 = 15/4.

Hmm, now I have an equation involving x², y², z², and their products. Maybe I can relate this to T = xy + yz + zx. Let's recall that (xy + yz + zx)^2 = x²y² + y²z² + z²x² + 2xyz(x + y + z). Wait, so:

(xy + yz + zx)^2 = x²y² + y²z² + z²x² + 2xyz(x + y + z).

We already know that xyz = 1/2, so 2xyz = 1. Therefore:

(xy + yz + zx)^2 = x²y² + y²z² + z²x² + (x + y + z).

But from the previous equation, we have x²y² + x²z² + y²z² + x² + y² + z² = 15/4. Let me rearrange that:

(x²y² + x²z² + y²z²) + (x² + y² + z²) = 15/4.

Let me denote S = x + y + z, and Q = x² + y² + z². Also, let me note that (xy + yz + zx) = T.

From the identity (x + y + z)^2 = x² + y² + z² + 2(xy + yz + zx), we have S² = Q + 2T.

T² = x²y² + y²z² + z²x² + (x + y + z).

Therefore, x²y² + y²z² + z²x² = T² - S.

(T² - S) + (S² - 2T) = 15/4.

Simplify this equation:

T² - S + S² - 2T = 15/4.

Let me rewrite that as S² - S + T² - 2T = 15/4.

This seems a bit complicated. Maybe I can express S in terms of T or vice versa. Alternatively, perhaps I can find a substitution or a way to combine these variables.

Alternatively, maybe symmetry can be used here. The problem is symmetric in x, y, z, so perhaps the maximum occurs when x = y = z. Let me check if that's possible.

Assume x = y = z. Then, from the first constraint: 2x³ = 1 ⇒ x³ = 1/2 ⇒ x = (1/2)^(1/3) = 2^(-1/3).

Then T = 3x². Let me compute T:

x = 2^(-1/3) ⇒ x² = 2^(-2/3). Therefore, T = 3 * 2^(-2/3) ≈ 3 / (2^(2/3)) ≈ 3 / 1.5874 ≈ 1.89.

MOD = 10**9 + 7

n, m = map(int, input().split())
broken = set()
for _ in range(m):
    a = int(input())
    broken.add(a)

dp = [0] * (n + 1)
dp[0] = 1  # Starting point

for i in range(1, n + 1):
    if i in broken:
        dp[i] = 0
    else:
        dp[i] = dp[i-1]  # Add ways from previous step
        if i >= 2:
            dp[i] += dp[i-2]  # Add ways from two steps back
        dp[i] %= MOD  # Apply modulo to prevent overflow

In Lagrange multipliers, when there are multiple constraints, we have to use gradients. The gradients of the objective function and the constraints must be linearly dependent. So, set up the system:

∇T = λ∇g + μ∇h,

where g(x,y,z) = 2xyz -1 =0 and h(x,y,z)=(x² +1)(y² +1)(z² +1) -5=0.

∇g = (2yz, 2xz, 2xy).

∇h: Let's compute derivative of h with respect to x:

h = (x² +1)(y² +1)(z² +1), so

∂h/∂x = 2x(y² +1)(z² +1).

Similarly,

∂h/∂y = 2y(x² +1)(z² +1),

∂h/∂z = 2z(x² +1)(y² +1).

So, the gradient ∇h = [2x(y² +1)(z² +1), 2y(x² +1)(z² +1), 2z(x² +1)(y² +1)].

Therefore, the Lagrange multiplier equations are:

y + z = λ*(2yz) + μ*(2x(y² +1)(z² +1)),

x + z = λ*(2xz) + μ*(2y(x² +1)(z² +1)),

x + y = λ*(2xy) + μ*(2z(x² +1)(y² +1)).

assistant: Now let me understand the problem clearly:

### 1.1 Problem Analysis

Looking at the issue description and the code, I can see:

1. **File mentioned**: `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/alert.py` line 98
2. **Current constraint**: The `update_progress` method only accepts values between 0 and 1
3. **Issue**: Now that tqdm is supported, it should be possible to support progress values that are not between 0 and 1
4. **Specific line**: Line 98-99 contains a validation that raises ValueError if progress is not in [0, 1]

### 1.2 Technical Details

- **Method name**: `update_progress` in `Alert` class
- **Variable**: `progress` parameter
- **Error message**: `ValueError(f"progress should be in [0, 1], {progress} given")`
- **File name**: `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/alert.py`

### 1.3 Problem Explanation

The current implementation of `update_progress` method in the `Alert` class strictly enforces that progress values must be between 0 and 1. However, since tqdm is now supported, the method should allow for progress values that can exceed this range, particularly when `total` is explicitly set in `tqdm_args`.

### 1.4 Steps to Reproduce

1. Create an Alert instance
2. Call `update_progress` with a value > 1 (e.g., 1.5)
3. Observe the ValueError being raised

### 1.5 Best Practices

- The fix should maintain backward compatibility
- When `total` is provided in tqdm_args, progress values can exceed 1
- The validation should be more flexible to accommodate tqdm's behavior

From the equation S² - S + T² - 2T -15/4=0, this is a quadratic in S:

S² - S + (T² - 2T -15/4) =0.

We can solve for S:

S = [1 ± sqrt(1 -4*(T² -2T -15/4)}]/2.

1 -4*(T² -2T -15/4) ≥0.

4T² -8T -16 ≤0 ⇒ Divide by 4: T² -2T -4 ≤0.

Solving T² -2T -4 =0. Discriminant: 4 +16=20. Solutions: [2 ± sqrt(20)]/2 = [2 ± 2*sqrt(5)]/2 = 1 ± sqrt(5). Thus, the inequality holds for T between 1 - sqrt(5) and 1 + sqrt(5). def get_bit(x, y, z, N):

    return ( (x-1)*N*N + (y-1)*N + (z-1) )

def get_axis(c):

    if c in ['R', 'L']:

        return 0

    elif c in ['U', 'D']:

        return 1

    else:  # 'F','b'

        return 2

def get_directions(prev_axis):

    allowed_axes = [a for a in [0,1,2] if a != prev_axis]

    res = []

    for a in allowed_axes:

        if a ==0:

            res += ['R', 'L']

        elif a ==1:

            res += ['U', 'D']

        else:

            res += ['F', 'B']

    return res

        # current_i is the seg idx being processed.

            # then need to process the movement.

            # compute each step of the movement.

            valid = True

            new_mask = mask

            current_x, current_y, current_z = pos

            new_positions = []

            for step in range(1, seg +1):

                new_x = current_x + dx * step

                new_y = current_y + dy * step

                new_z = current_z + dz * step

                if not (1 <= new_x <=N and 1<= new_y <=N and 1<= new_z <=N):

                    valid = False

                    break

                # check if already in mask

                coord_bit = get_bit(new_x, new_y, new_z, N)

                if (new_mask & (1<< coord_bit)) !=0:

                    valid = False

                    break

                new_mask |= (1 << coord_bit)

                new_positions.append( (new_x, new_y, new_z) )

            if not valid:

                continue

            # if all steps are valid, then proceed

            new_pos_last = (new_x, new_y, new_z)

            new_axis = get_axis(candidate)

            new_path = path.copy() + [candidate]

            backtrack(new_pos_last, new_mask, new_path, i+1, new_axis )

def get_delta( d ):

    if d == 'R': return (1,0,0)

    elif d == 'L': return (-1,0,0)

    elif d == 'U': return (0,1,0)

    elif d == 'D': return (0,-1,0)

    elif d == 'F': return (0,0,1)

    elif d == 'B': return (0,0,-1)

y=1/2: 4*(1/8) -15*(1/2) +10=0.5 -7.5 +10=3≠0.

y=5/2: 4*(125/8) -15*(5/2) +10= 62.5 -37.5 +10=35≠0.

So no rational roots. So maybe use the depressed cubic formula. The general solution for t³ + pt + q=0 is:

t = cube_root(-q/2 + sqrt((q/2)^2 + (p/3)^3)) + cube_root(-q/2 - sqrt((q/2)^2 + (p/3)^3)).

Here, equation is 4y³ -15y +10=0. But we need to write it as y³ + Py + Q=0. Divide by 4:

y³ - (15/4)y + 10/4=0 ⇒ y³ -3.75y +2.5=0.

So, P= -15/4, Q=5/2.

Discriminant D= (Q/2)^2 + (P/3)^3.

Compute:

(Q/2)^2= (5/4)^2=25/16,

(P/3)^3= (-15/4 /3)^3= (-5/4)^3= -125/64.

Thus, D=25/16 -125/64= (100/64 -125/64)= -25/64 <0.

Since D <0, the equation has three real roots, expressed using trigonometric functions.

The formula for this case is:

y = 2*sqrt(-P/3) * cos(theta + 2kπ/3), where k=0,1,2,

and theta = arccos( -Q/(2*(-P/3)^(3/2)) )

Compute:

-P/3 = (15/4)/3=5/4. So sqrt(-P/3)=sqrt(5/4)=sqrt(5)/2.

theta=arccos( -Q/(2*( (-P/3)^(3/2) )) )

First, compute denominator:2*( (-P/3)^(3/2))=2*(5/4)^(3/2).

(5/4)^(3/2)= (5/4)*sqrt(5/4)= (5/4)*(sqrt(5)/2)=5 sqrt(5)/8.

So denominator=2*(5 sqrt(5)/8)=5 sqrt(5)/4.

-Q= -5/2.

So theta=arccos( (-5/2)/(5 sqrt(5)/4) )= arccos( (-5/2)*(4/(5 sqrt(<#sep#> Python, when using append and then popping, perhaps it can be done. 

Wait this suggests, path is modified in place. So:

for candidate in candidates:

    new_path = path + [candidate]

    and the function is passed new_path. But this creates copies each time. For recursive calls with long paths (like 64 directions?), this can be slow.

Alternatively, use a list that is reused, appended, and then popped.

 Here’s how:

def backtrack(pos, mask, path, i, prev_axis):

    if i == N ...:

    ...

    for candidate in get_candidate_directions(...):

        ... compute stuff.

        if valid is False:

            continue

        # After computing new_mask and new_pos_last:

        path.append( candidate )

        backtrack( ... new_pos_last ... new_mask, path, ... )

        path.pop()

This way, the path is modified in place, and we avoid making copies.

Thus, this may be more efficient.

 The initial call would have an empty path.

I think this would be better.

def get_bit(x, y, z, N):

    return (x-1)*N*N + (y-1)*N + (z-1)

def get_delta(d):

    if d == 'R':

        return (1,0,0)

    if d == 'L':

        return (-1,0,0)

    if d == 'U':

        return (0,1,0)

    if d == 'D':

        return (0,-1,0)

    if d == 'F':

        return (0,0,1)

    if d == 'B':

        return (0,0,-1)

def get_axis(d):

    if d in ['R','L']:

        return 0

    elif d in ['U','D']:

        return 1

    else:

        return 2

def get_candidate_directions(prev_axis, current_i):

    if current_i ==0:

        # first direction must be R/U/F

        return ['R', 'U', 'F']

    else:

        allowed_axes = []

        for a in [0,1,2]:

            if a != prev_axis:

                allowed_axes.append(a)

        res = []

        for a in allowed_axes:

            if a ==0:

                res.append('R')

                res.append('L')

            elif a ==1:

                res.append('U')

                res.append('D')

            else:

                res.append('F')

                res.append('B')

        return res

def solve(N, segments):

    results = []

    from collections import deque

    # initial position (1,1,1)

    INIT_X, INIT_Y, INIT_Z = (1,1,1)

    INIT_BIT = get_bit(INIT_X, INIT_Y, INIT_Z, N)

    initial_mask = (1 << INIT_BIT)

    def backtrack( pos, mask, path, i, prev_axis):

        if i == len(segments):

            if pos == (N,N,N) and (mask == ( (1 << (N*N*N)) ) -1 ):

                results.append( ''.join(path) )

            return

        current_segment_length = segments[i]

        for candidate in get_candidate_directions(prev_axis, i):

            # get direction parameters

            dx, dy, dz = get_delta(candidate)

            current_x, current_y, current_z = pos

            new_mask = mask

            valid = True

            new_positions = []

            final_pos = None

            for step in range(1, current_segment_length +1):

                new_x = current_x + dx * step

                new_y = current_y + dy * step

                new_z = current_z + dz * step

                if not (1 <= new_x <= N and 1 <= new_y <=N and 1 <= new_z <= N ):

                    valid = False

                    break

                bit = get_bit(new_x, new_y, new_z, N)

                if (new_mask & (1 << bit )):

                    valid = False

                    break

                new_mask = new_mask | (1 << bit)

                final_pos = (new_x, new_y, new_z)

            if not valid:

                continue

            new_axis = get_axis(candidate)

            # path.append( candidate )

            backtrack( final_pos, new_mask, path + [candidate], i+1, new_axis )

    backtrack( ( INIT_X, INIT_Y, INIT_Z ), initial_mask, [], 0, -1 )

    results.sort()

    return results

import sys

def is_convex(points):
    cross = []
    for i in range(4):
        p = points[i]
        q = points[(i+1)%4]
        r = points[(i+2)%4]
        dx1 = q[0] - p[0]
        dy1 = q[1] - p[1]
        dx2 = r[0] - q[0]
        dy2 = r[1] - q[1]
        cross_val = dx1 * dy2 - dy1 * dx2
        cross.append(cross_val)
    if all(c >= 0 for c in cross) or all(c <= 0 for c in cross):
        return "YES"
    else:
        return "NO"

def get_axis(d):
    if d in ['R','L']:
        return 0
    elif d in ['U','D']:
        return 1
    else:
        return 2

def get_candidate_directions(prev_axis, current_i):
    if current_i ==0:
        return ['R', 'U', 'F']
    else:
        allowed_axes = []
        for a in [0,1,2]:
            if a != prev_axis:
                allowed_axes.append(a)
        res = []
        for a in allowed_axes:
            if a ==0:
                res.append('R')
                res.append('L')
            elif a ==1:
                res.append('U')
                res.append('D')
            else:
                res.append('F')
                res.append('B')
        return res

Given the complexity, perhaps the only real solution is t=1, which corresponds to x²=1, x=1. Hence, when x=1, y=1, z=1/2, we get T=2. Maybe this is the only real solution and hence the maximum T.

Alternatively, since the quartic equation had t=1 as a root, and others are complex, then x=1 is the only real solution, leading to T=2. Therefore, the maximum value of T is 2.

But to confirm, let me check if there are other real solutions. Suppose the cubic 4t³ +12t² -3t -1=0 has three real roots. For that, since its discriminant is negative, it has three real roots. But solving them requires complex numbers, but maybe approximate them.

Let me plug in t=0.5:

4*(0.125) +12*(0.25) -3*(0.5) -1=0.5+3-1.5-1=1>0.

t=0:

0 +0 -0 -1=-1<0.

Thus, there's a root between 0 and 0.5.

t=0.3:

4*(0.027)+12*(0.09)-3*(0.3)-1=0.108+1.08-0.9-1= -0.712<0.

t=0.4:

4*(0.064)+12*(0.16)-3*(0.4)-1=0.256+1.92-1.2-1=0.976-2.2= -1.224<0.

t=0.45:

4*(0.091125)+12*(0.2025)-3*(0.45)-1=0.3645+2.43-1.35-1≈0.3645+2.43=2.7945 -2.35≈0.4445>0.

Thus, a root between 0.4 and 0.45.

Similarly, t=1 is a root, as found before. Also, since the leading coefficient is positive and the cubic tends to +infty as t→+infty and -infty as t→-infty, there might be another negative root.

So, potentially three real roots: t≈0.42, t=1, and a negative root. But since x² is always positive, only t=1 and t≈0.42 are valid. So, two possible solutions.

If x²=1, then x=1, y=1, z=1/2. T=2.

If x²≈0.42, then x=sqrt(0.42)≈0.648, so y=x≈0.648, z=1/(2x²)≈1/(2*0.42)≈1.1905.

Compute T=xy +yz +zx= x² +2xz=0.42 +2*0.648*1.1905≈0.42 +2*0.771≈0.42+1.542≈1.962, which is less than 2.

So T is lower here. Similarly, for the negative root (which is not applicable since x,y,z>0).

Therefore, the maximum T=2 is achieved at x=y=1, z=1/2.

Hence, the maximum value of T is 2.
</think>To find the maximum value of \( T = xy + yz + zx \) given the constraints \( 2xyz = 1 \) and \( (x^2 + 1)(y^2 + 1)(z^2 + 1) = 5 \), we proceed as follows:

1. **Rewrite the constraints:**
   \[
   xyz = \frac{1}{2}
   \]
   \[
   (x^2 + 1)(y^2 + 1)(z^2 + 1) = 5
   \]

2. **Expand the second constraint:**
   \[
   (x^2 + 1)(y^2 + 1)(z^2 + 1) = x^2y^2z^2 + x^2y^2 + x^2z^2 + y^2z^2 + x^2 + y^2 + z^2 + 1
   \]
   Since \( x^2y^2z^2 = \left( \frac{1}{2} \right)^2 = \frac{1}{4} \), we have:
   \[
   \frac{1}{4} + x^2y^2 + x^2z^2 + y^2z^2 + x^2 + y^2 + z^2 + 1 = 5
   \]
   Simplifying, we get:
   \[
   x^2y^2 + x^2z^2 + y^2z^2 + x^2 + y^2 + z^2 = \frac{15}{4}
   \]

3. **Express \( T \) in terms of \( S \) and \( Q \):**
   Let \( S = x + y + z \) and \( Q = x^2 + y^2 + z^2 \).
   \[
   T = xy + yz + zx
   \]
   We know:
   \[
   (x + y + z)^2 = x^2 + y^2 + z^2 + 2(xy + yz + zx)
   \]
   \[
   S^2 = Q + 2T
   \]
   Therefore:
   \[
   Q = S^2 - 2T
   \]

There are n computers for participants, the i-th of which has power equal to positive integer pi. At the same time there are m sockets available, the j-th of which has power euqal to positive integer sj. It is possible to connect the i-th computer to the j-th socket if and only if their powers are the same: pi = sj. It is allowed to connect no more than one computer to one socket. Thus, if the powers of all computers and sockets are distinct, then no computer can be connected to any of the sockets. 

In order to fix the situation professor Puch Williams urgently ordered a wagon of adapters — power splitters. Each adapter has one plug and one socket with a voltage divider between them. After plugging an adapter to a socket with power x, the power on the adapter's socket becomes equal to <image>, it means that it is equal to the socket's power divided by two with rounding up, for example <image> and <image>.

Each adapter can be used only once. It is possible to connect several adapters in a chain plugging the first to a socket. For example, if two adapters are plugged one after enother to a socket with power 10, it becomes possible to connect one computer with power 3 to this socket.

The organizers should install adapters so that it will be possible to supply with electricity the maximum number of computers c at the same time. If there are several possible connection configurations, they want to find the one that uses the minimum number of adapters u to connect c computers.

Help organizers calculate the maximum number of connected computers c and the minimum number of adapters u needed for this.

The wagon of adapters contains enough of them to do the task. It is guaranteed that it's possible to connect at least one computer.
Input

The first line contains two integers n and m (1 ≤ n, m ≤ 200 000) — the number of computers and the number of sockets.

The second line contains n integers p1, p2, ..., pn (1 ≤ pi ≤ 109) — the powers of the computers. 

The third line contains m integers s1, s2, ..., sm (1 ≤ si ≤ 109) — the power of the sockets. 

Output

In the first line print two numbers c and u — the maximum number of computers which can at the same time be connected to electricity and the minimum number of adapters needed to connect c computers.

In the second line print m integers a1, a2, ..., am (0 ≤ ai ≤ 109), where ai equals the number of adapters orginizers need to plug into the i-th socket. The sum of all ai should be equal to u.

In third line print n integers b1, b2, ..., bn (0 ≤ bi ≤ m), where the bj-th equals the number of the socket which the j-th computer should be connected to. bj = 0 means that the j-th computer should not be connected to any socket. All bj that are different from 0 should be distinct. The power of the j-th computer should be equal to the power of the socket bj after plugging in abj adapters. The number of non-zero bj should be equal to c.

If there are multiple answers, print any of them.

Examples

Input

2 2
1 1
2 2


Output

2 2
1 1
1 2


Input

2 1
2 100
99


Output

1 6
6
1 0

import sys
from collections import defaultdict

def main():
    N = int(sys.stdin.readline())
    counts = defaultdict(int)
    initial_max = 0

    for _ in range(N):
        a, b = map(int, sys.stdin.readline().split())
        k = a + b
        counts[k] += 1
        if k > initial_max:
            initial_max = k

    current_max = initial_max
    k = 0

    while k <= current_max:
        cnt = counts.get(k, 0)
        carry = cnt // 2
        rem = cnt % 2
        counts[k] = rem
        if carry > 0:
            counts[k + 1] += carry
            if k + 1 > current_max:
                current_max = k + 1
        k += 1

    result = []
    for exp in sorted(counts.keys()):
        if counts[exp] == 1:
            result.append((exp, 0))
    
    for x, y in result:
        print(x, y)

def subtract_one(s):
    s_list = list(s)
    i = len(s_list) - 1
    while i >= 0 and s_list[i] == '0':
        s_list[i] = '9'
        i -= 1
    if i < 0:
        return '0'
    s_list[i] = str(int(s_list[i]) - 1)
    new_s = ''.join(s_list).lstrip('0')
    return new_s if new_s else '0'

def compute(s, m):
    if m == 0:
        return 0
    X = list(map(int, s))
    n = len(X)
    from functools import lru_cache

    @lru_cache(maxsize=None)
    def dp(pos, mod, prev_digit, last_dir, tight, started):
        if pos == n:
            return 1 if (started and mod == 0) else 0
        limit = X[pos] if tight else 9
        total = 0
        for d in range(0, limit + 1):
            new_tight = tight and (d == limit)
            new_started = started or (d != 0)
            if not new_started:
                new_mod = 0
                new_pd = -1
                new_ld = 0
                res = dp(pos + 1, new_mod, new_pd, new_ld, new_tight, new_started)
                total = (total + res) % 10000
            else:
                if not started:
                    new_mod = d % m
                    new_pd = d
                    new_ld = 0
                    res = dp(pos + 1, new_mod, new_pd, new_ld, new_tight, new_started)
                    total = (total + res) % 10000
                else:
                    if d == prev_digit:
                        continue
                    current_dir = 1 if d > prev_digit else -1
                    if last_dir == 0:
                        new_ld = current_dir
                        new_mod_val = (mod * 10 + d) % m
                        res = dp(pos + 1, new_mod_val, d, new_ld, new_tight, new_started)
                        total = (total + res) % 10000
                    else:
                        if current_dir == last_dir:
                            continue
                        new_ld = current_dir
                        new_mod_val = (mod * 10 + d) % m
                        res = dp(pos + 1, new_mod_val, d, new_ld, new_tight, new_started)
                        total = (total + res) % 10000
        return total % 10000

    return dp(0, 0, -1, 0, True, False)

A = input().strip()
B = input().strip()
M = int(input())
lower = subtract_one(A)
upper_count = compute(B, M)
lower_count = compute(lower, M) if lower != '0' else 0
ans = (upper_count - lower_count) % 10000

import heapq

class Edge:
    def __init__(self, to, rev, capacity, cost):
        self.to = to
        self.rev = rev
        self.capacity = capacity
        self.cost = cost

def add_edge(adj, fr, to, capacity, cost):
    adj[fr].append(Edge(to, len(adj[to]), capacity, cost))
    adj[to].append(Edge(fr, len(adj[fr])-1, 0, -cost))

def min_cost_flow(adj, s, t, maxf):
    n = len(adj)
    res = 0.0
    h = [0.0] * n
    prevv = [0] * n
    preve = [0] * n
    INF = float('inf')

    flow = 0
    while flow < maxf:
        dist = [INF] * n
        dist[s] = 0.0
        heap = []
        heapq.heappush(heap, (0.0, s))
        while heap:
            d, u = heapq.heappop(heap)
            if d > dist[u]:
                continue
            for i, e in enumerate(adj[u]):
                if e.capacity > 0 and dist[e.to] > d + e.cost + h[u] - h[e.to]:
                    dist[e.to] = d + e.cost + h[u] - h[e.to]
                    prevv[e.to] = u
                    preve[e.to] = i
                    heapq.heappush(heap, (dist[e.to], e.to))
        if dist[t] == INF:
            return None
        for v in range(n):
            if dist[v] < INF:
                h[v] += dist[v]
        d_flow = maxf - flow
        v = t
        while v != s:
            d_flow = min(d_flow, adj[prevv[v]][preve[v]].capacity)
            v = prevv[v]
        flow += d_flow
        res += d_flow * (h[t] - h[s])
        v = t
        while v != s:
            e = adj[prevv[v]][preve[v]]
            e.capacity -= d_flow
            adj[v][e.rev].capacity += d_flow
            v = prevv[v]
    return res

def solve_gauss(aug, n):
    for i in range(n):
        aug[i] = [float(x) for x in aug[i]]
    for col in range(n):
        pivot = -1
        max_val = -1.0
        for row in range(col, n):
            current = abs(aug[row][col])
            if current > max_val:
                max_val = current
                pivot = row
        if pivot == -1 or max_val == 0:
            return None
        aug[col], aug[pivot] = aug[pivot], aug[col]
        divisor = aug[col][col]
        for j in range(col, n+1):
            aug[col][j] /= divisor
        for i in range(n):
            if i != col and abs(aug[i][col]) > 1e-10:
                factor = aug[i][col]
                for j in range(col, n+1):
                    aug[i][j] -= factor * aug[col][j]
    return [row[n] for row in aug]

def main():
    import sys
    input = sys.stdin.read().split()
    ptr = 0
    T = int(input[ptr])
    ptr +=1
    for _ in range(T):
        N = int(input[ptr])
        s = int(input[ptr+1])
        t = int(input[ptr+2])
        F = int(input[ptr+3])
        ptr +=4
        aug = []
        for _ in range(N):
            row = list(map(int, input[ptr:ptr+N+1]))
            ptr +=N+1
            aug.append(row)
        aug_copy = [r.copy() for r in aug]
        solution = solve_gauss(aug_copy, N)
        if solution is None:
            for i in range(N):
                Mi = int(input[ptr])
                ptr +=1
                ptr += Mi
                ptr += Mi
            print("impossible")
            continue
        T_list = solution
        adj = [[] for _ in range(N)]
        for i in range(N):
            Mi = int(input[ptr])
            ptr +=1
            if Mi ==0:
                ptr += 2*Mi
                continue
            di_j = list(map(int, input[ptr:ptr+Mi]))
            ptr +=Mi
            fi_j = list(map(int, input[ptr:ptr+Mi]))
            ptr +=Mi
            for j in range(Mi):
                fr = i
                to = di_j[j]
                capacity = fi_j[j]
                cost = abs(T_list[fr] - T_list[to])
                add_edge(adj, fr, to, capacity, cost)
        result = min_cost_flow(adj, s, t, F)
        if result is not None:
            print("{0:.10f}".format(result))
        else:
            print("impossible")

Okay, so I need to figure out for which functions f and g the equation f(g(x)) equals g(f(x)) holds true. Hmm, let's start by understanding what this means. The equation is saying that composing f after g is the same as composing g after f. That is, applying f to the result of g(x) gives the same result as applying g to the result of f(x). 

First, maybe I can think of some examples where this is true. Let's take simple functions. For instance, if both f and g are linear functions, like f(x) = ax + b and g(x) = cx + d. Let's see if their compositions commute. 

So f(g(x)) would be a(cx + d) + b = acx + ad + b. On the other hand, g(f(x)) would be c(ax + b) + d = acx + cb + d. For these to be equal for all x, the constants must satisfy ad + b = cb + d. So, that's a condition on a, b, c, d. For example, if a=1 and c=1, then we have d + b = b + d, which is always true. So, if both functions are linear with slope 1, then they commute. But also, there might be other combinations. Suppose a=2, c=3. Then ad + b = 3b + d. Let's see: 2d + b = 3b + d → 2d - d = 3b - b → d = 2b. So if d is twice b, then even with different slopes, they might commute. Interesting. So in the linear case, there's a condition on the coefficients for f and g to commute.

But when constants are added, the condition becomes ad + b = cb + d. So to satisfy that, we can rearrange:

ad + b = cb + d  
ad - d = cb - b  
d(a - 1) = b(c - 1)

So, if a = 1 and c = 1, then the left and right sides are 0, so any b and d would work. So if f(x) = x + b and g(x) = x + d, then f(g(x)) = x + d + b and g(f(x)) = x + b + d, which are the same. Thus for any p_i that is part of the sequence of s, then steps can be computed as path length from s to p_i.

So for example, socket s=8: steps down is 8→4 → 2 →1. To reach 4 would take 1 step, 2 steps for 2, etc.

Thus, let's think of each s generating a path that decreases to 1. Each value along that path can be used to connect to a computer. 

Thus, for a computer p_i, the socket s_j can be suitable if p_i is on the path of s_j, and among all such sockets, the minimal steps is the minimum possible.

So to choose the best s_j (with least steps) for p_i, and only use each s_j once.

This seems to go back to the problem of for each p_i, find all s_j ≥ p_i which have p_i on their path (i.e., p_i appears in their sequence of steps), and then choose the best s_j where the steps required is minimal.

    55	class Alert(v.Alert, SepalWidget):
    56	    """
    57	    A custom Alert widget. It is used as the output of all processes in the framework.
    58	    In the voila interfaces, print statement will not be displayed.
    59	    Instead use the sw.Alert method to provide information to the user.
    60	    It's hidden by default.
    61	
    62	    Args:
    63	        type\_ (str, optional): The color of the Alert
    64	        kwargs (optional): any parameter from a v.Alert. If set, 'type' will be overwritten.
    65	    """
    66	
    67	    def __init__(self, type_="info", **kwargs):
    68	
    69	        # set default parameters
    70	        kwargs["text"] = kwargs.pop("text", True)
    71	        kwargs["type"] = set_type(type_)
    72	        kwargs["class_"] = kwargs.pop("class_", "mt-5")
    73	
    74	        # call the constructor
    75	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
    76	
    77	        self.hide()
    78	        self.progress_output = Output()
    79	        self.progress_bar = None
    80	
    81	    def update_progress(self, progress, msg="Progress", **tqdm_args):
    82	        """
    83	        Update the Alert message with a tqdm progress bar.
    84	
    85	        Args:
    86	            progress (float): the progress status in float [0, 1]
    87	            msg (str, optionnal): The message to use before the progress bar
    88	
    89	        Return:
    90	            self
    91	        """
    92	
    93	        # show the alert
    94	        self.show()
    95	
    96	        # cast the progress to float
    97	        progress = float(progress)
    98	        if not (0 <= progress <= 1):
    99	            raise ValueError(f"progress should be in [0, 1], {progress} given")
   100	
   101	        # Prevent adding multiple times
   102	        if self.progress_output not in self.children:
   103	
   104	            self.children = [self.progress_output]
   105	
   106	            tqdm_args["bar_format"] = tqdm_args.pop(
   107	                "bar_format", "{l_bar}{bar}{n_fmt}/{total_fmt}"
   108	            )
   109	            tqdm_args["dynamic_ncols"] = tqdm_args.pop("dynamic_ncols", tqdm_args)
   110	            tqdm_args["total"] = tqdm_args.pop("total", 100)
   111	            tqdm_args["desc"] = tqdm_args.pop("desc", msg)
   112	            tqdm_args["colour"] = tqdm_args.pop("tqdm_args", getattr(color, self.type))
   113	
   114	            with self.progress_output:
   115	                self.progress_output.clear_output()
   116	                self.progress_bar = tqdm(**tqdm_args)
   117	                self.progress_bar.container.children[0].add_class(f"{self.type}--text")
   118	                self.progress_bar.container.children[2].add_class(f"{self.type}--text")
   119	
   120	                # Initialize bar
   121	                self.progress_bar.update(0)
   122	
   123	        self.progress_bar.update(progress * 100 - self.progress_bar.n)
   124	
   125	        if progress == 1:
   126	            self.progress_bar.close()
   127	
   128	        return
   129	
   130	    def add_msg(self, msg, type_="info"):
   131	        """
   132	        Add a message in the alert by replacing all the existing one.
   133	        The color can also be changed dynamically
   134	
   135	        Args:
   136	            msg (str): the message to display
   137	            type\_ (str, optional): the color to use in the widget
   138	
   139	        Return:
   140	            self
   141	        """
   142	        self.show()
   143	        self.type = set_type(type_)
   144	        self.children = [v.Html(tag="p", children=[msg])]
   145	
   146	        return self
   147	
   148	    def add_live_msg(self, msg, type_="info"):
   149	        """
   150	        Add a message in the alert by replacing all the existing one.
   151	        Also add the timestamp of the display.
   152	        The color can also be changed dynamically
   153	
   154	        Args:
   155	            msg (str): the message to display
   156	            type\_ (str, optional): the color to use in the widget
   157	
   158	        Return:
   159	            self
   160	        """
   161	
   162	        current_time = datetime.now().strftime("%Y/%m/%d, %H:%M:%S")
   163	
   164	        self.show()
   165	        self.type = set_type(type_)
   166	        self.children = [
   167	            v.Html(tag="p", children=["[{}]".format(current_time)]),
   168	            v.Html(tag="p", children=[msg]),
   169	        ]
   170	
   171	        return self
   172	
   173	    def append_msg(self, msg, section=False, type_="info"):
   174	        """
   175	        Append a message in a new parragraph, with or without divider
   176	
   177	        Args:
   178	            msg (str): the message to display
   179	            section (bool, optional): add a Divider before the added message
   180	
   181	        Return:
   182	            self
   183	        """
   184	
   185	        self.show()
   186	        self.type = type_
   187	
   188	        if len(self.children):
   189	            current_children = self.children[:]
   190	            if section:
   191	                # As the list is mutable, and the trait is only triggered when
   192	                # the children is changed, so have to create a copy.
   193	                divider = Divider(class_="my-4", style="opacity: 0.22")
   194	
   195	                # link Alert type with divider type
   196	                directional_link((self, "type"), (divider, "type_"))
   197	                current_children.extend([divider, v.Html(tag="p", children=[msg])])
   198	                self.children = current_children
   199	
   200	            else:
   201	                current_children.append(v.Html(tag="p", children=[msg]))
   202	                self.children = current_children
   203	        else:
   204	            self.add_msg(msg)
   205	
   206	        return self
   207	
   208	    def remove_last_msg(self):
   209	        """
   210	        Remove the last msg printed in the Alert widget
   211	
   212	        Return:
   213	            self
   214	        """
   215	
   216	        if len(self.children) > 1:
   217	            current_children = self.children[:]
   218	            self.children = current_children[:-1]
   219	        else:
   220	            self.reset()
   221	
   222	        return self
   223	
   224	    def reset(self):
   225	        """
   226	        Empty the messages and hide it
   227	
   228	        Return:
   229	            self
   230	        """
   231	
   232	        self.children = [""]
   233	        self.hide()
   234	
   235	        return self

import sys

def main():
    for line in sys.stdin:
        line = line.strip()
        if not line:
            continue
        if line == '0':
            break
        N = int(line)
        days = []
        valid = True
        # Read N days and check validity
        for _ in range(N):
            # Read next non-empty line
            while True:
                curr_line = sys.stdin.readline()
                if not curr_line:
                    break
                curr_line = curr_line.strip()
                if curr_line:
                    break
            nums = list(map(int, curr_line.split()))
            if len(nums) != 16:
                valid = False
            zeros = [i for i, val in enumerate(nums) if val == 0]
            if len(zeros) != 4:
                valid = False
            else:
                rows = [i // 4 for i in zeros]
                cols = [i % 4 for i in zeros]
                r_min, r_max = min(rows), max(rows)
                c_min, c_max = min(cols), max(cols)
                if (r_max - r_min != 1) or (c_max - c_min != 1):
                    valid = False
                else:
                    expected = set()
                    for r in [r_min, r_min + 1]:
                        for c in [c_min, c_min + 1]:
                            expected.add(r * 4 + c)
                    if set(zeros) != expected:
                        valid = False
                    else:
                        cloud = (r_min, c_min)
                        days.append(cloud)
            # Check first day's cloud position
            if _ == 0:
                if days and days[0] != (1, 2):
                    valid = False
            if not valid:
                # Skip remaining days
                for __ in range(_ + 1, N):
                    sys.stdin.readline()
                break
        if not valid:
            print(0)
            continue
        # Check movement between days
        for i in range(1, len(days)):
            prev_r, prev_c = days[i-1]
            curr_r, curr_c = days[i]
            dr = curr_r - prev_r
            dc = curr_c - prev_c
            if dr == 0 and dc == 0:
                continue
            if dr == 0 and dc in (-2, -1, 1, 2):
                continue
            elif dc == 0 and dr in (-2, -1, 1, 2):
                continue
            else:
                valid = False
                break
        if not valid:
            print(0)
            continue
        # Check consecutive dry days
        dry_days = [0] * 16
        valid = True
        for cloud_r, cloud_c in days:
            for area in range(16):
                row = area // 4
                col = area % 4
                if (row >= cloud_r) and (row <= cloud_r + 1) and (col >= cloud_c) and (col <= cloud_c + 1):
                    dry_days[area] = 0
                else:
                    dry_days[area] += 1
                    if dry_days[area] > 6:
                        valid = False
            if not valid:
                break
        print(1 if valid else 0)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()<#sep#> it's not easy to characterize all pairs of commuting functions. There are some specific cases, like translations (f(x) = x + a, g(x) = x + b), which commute because addition is commutative. Similarly, scaling functions (f(x) = ax, g(x) = bx) commute because multiplication is commutative. Also, functions that are iterations of each other might commute, like f(f(x)) commutes with f(x), but that's trivial.

Another example is when one function is a power function and the other is a linear function. Let's take f(x) = x + 1 and g(x) = 2x. Then f(g(x)) = 2x + 1, and g(f(x)) = 2(x + 1) = 2x + 2. These are not equal, so they don't commute. So linear functions with scaling and translation only commute if they meet the earlier conditions. 

Alternatively, if we take f and g as rotations in the plane. Rotations commute if their angles add up to the same in either order, which is always true because rotation angles add. So composing two rotations is commutative. But this is a different context, functions from the plane to itself.

But in the real line, perhaps the commuting functions are limited. Maybe the only continuous solutions are linear functions of the form f(x) = x + c and g(x) = x + d, or scaling functions f(x) = ax and g(x) = bx, provided that they satisfy the condition for scaling plus translation as we saw earlier.

Alternatively, maybe functions that are exponentials with the same base, like f(x) = a^x and g(x) = a^x, then composing them would give a^(a^x), which is the same in both orders. Wait, no: f(g(x)) = a^{a^x}, and g(f(x)) = a^{a^x}, so they are the same. But if the bases are different, like 2 and 3, then 2^{3^x} vs 3^{2^x}, which are different. So same-base exponentials commute.

Another thought: if f and g are both the identity function, then they commute. Or if one is the identity and the other is any function. Wait, if f is the identity (f(x) = x), then f(g(x)) = g(x) and g(f(x)) = g(x), so they commute. Similarly, if g is the identity, same thing. So if one of the functions is the identity function, they commute with any function. Wait, no. Wait, if f is the identity, then f(g(x)) = g(x), and g(f(x)) = g(x), so yes, equal. Similarly, if g is the identity, then f(g(x)) = f(x), and g(f(x)) = f(x). So yes, identity function commutes with any function. That's a case. 

Similarly, constant functions. Suppose f(x) = c and g(x) = d. Then f(g(x)) = c, and g(f(x)) = d. So unless c = d, they are different. But if both f and g are constant functions with the same constant, then f∘g and g∘f are both equal to that constant. So constant functions commute if they are the same constant. But if they are different constants, they don't. 

But perhaps more generally, if f is a constant function, say f(x) = c, then f(g(x)) = c, and g(f(x)) = g(c). For these to be equal, we must have g(c) = c. So if f is a constant function c, then g must fix c, i.e., g(c) = c. Similarly, if g is a constant function d, then f(d) must equal d. So in that case, f and g commute if f(d) = d and g is the constant function d, or vice versa.

3. One is a scaling and the other is a translation, provided that the scaling factor is 1 (so effectively, translations commute with identity scalings).
4. Both are linear functions f(x) = ax + b and g(x) = cx + d satisfying ad + b = cb + d.
5. Both are exponential functions with the same base.
6. One is the identity function and the other is arbitrary.
7. Both are powers functions with exponents that commute under multiplication (like x^m and x^n, since (x^m)^n = (x^n)^m = x^{mn}).
8. Inverses of each other, since f(g(x)) = x and g(f(x)) = x.

2. For the remaining sockets and computers, try to connect them using some steps.

 **Step 2: Handling Non-Exact Matches**

Now, for remaining computers p_i (not already matched), and remaining sockets s_j (those not used in exact matches), find pairs (p_i, s_j) such that s_j can reach p_i with some steps, and choose these in a way that maximizes c (number connected), and under that choice, the steps used are minimum.

 To find this, perhaps we can proceed with a priority approach.

 For each p_i:

 We need to find a socket s_j >= p_i, that can reach p_i with minimal steps, and is not already used.

 To compute the minimal steps for p_i and s_j is the core problem.

 Thus, perhaps the approach is as follows:

 a. Sort the remaining computers in non-increasing order (so largest p_i first).

 b. Sort the remaining sockets in non-increasing order (so largest s_j first). 

 c. For each p_i (from largest to smallest), look for the best available s_j that can reach p_i with least steps, and hasn't been used yet.

 d. Pick the s_j that offers the least steps, and connect them.

 e. Record the number of steps for that s_j, track the sockets used.

 This way, we can maximize the count (since we process larger p_i first, which require larger s_j and thus not "lock up" smaller s_j for smaller p_i which can use less steps).

 The challenge is the steps.

 How to efficiently find for each p_i the minimal steps s_j which can be used?

**Computing Minimal Steps for a Given p and Socket Pool:**

To compute this, perhaps for each p_i, and s_j larger than or equal to p_i, we can represent the sockets in a way that allows us to quickly find the first s_j >= p which can go to p_i in minimal steps. 

 We need for all s_j in some data structure, for a given p, find the socket s_j which is the smallest s_j >= p and can be used reaching p_i in the smallest number of steps.

The steps needed is the number of steps from s down to p via ceil division steps.

 The path's steps form a sequence.

The predecessors of p are those numbers s such that ceil(s/2) equals p. Those are s from p to ~2p.

The number of steps required to get from s down to p is exactly log2(s/p), but dependent on the intermediate steps.

To compute the steps between s and p:

We can compute it iteratively. 

Start with current = s, count =0.

 current = ceil(current/2)

 

If current ==p → return count.

Else → return infinity.

This algorithms runs in O(log s) time. 

Asymptotically manageable for a small number of lookups.

 Then choose that s_j with the minimal steps (and not used yet).

   320	
   321	    btn_close = None
   322	    "v.Btn: the closing btn of the banner"
   323	
   324	    def __init__(self, msg="", type_="info", id_=None, persistent=True, **kwargs):
   325	
   326	        # compute the type and default to "info" if it's not existing
   327	        type_ = set_type(type_)
   328	
   329	        # create the closing btn

   331	            small=True, text=True, children=[ms.widgets.banner.close]
   332	        )
   333	
   334	        # compute timeout based on the persistent and timeout parameter
   335	        computed_timeout = 0 if persistent is True else self.get_timeout(msg)
   336	
   337	        kwargs["color"] = kwargs.pop("color", type_)
   338	        kwargs["transition"] = kwargs.pop("transition", "scroll-x-transition")
   339	        kwargs["attributes"] = {"id": id_}
   340	        kwargs["v_model"] = kwargs.pop("v_model", True)
   341	        kwargs["timeout"] = kwargs.pop("timeout", False) or computed_timeout
   342	        kwargs["top"] = True
   343	        kwargs["vertical"] = True
   344	        kwargs["children"] = [msg] + [self.btn_close]

   346	
   347	        super().__init__(**kwargs)
   348	
   349	        self.btn_close.on_event("click", self.close)
   350	
   351	    def close(self, *args):
   352	        """Close button event to close snackbar alert"""
   353	        self.v_model = False
   354	
   355	        return
   356	
   357	    def get_timeout(self, text):
   358	        """
   359	        Calculate timeout in miliseconds to read the message
   360	
   361	        Args:
   362	            text (str): the text displayed in the banner to adapt the duration of the timeout
   363	
   364	        Returns
   365	            (int): the duration of the timeout in milliseconds
   366	        """
   367	
   368	        wpm = 180  # readable words per minute
   369	        word_length = 5  # standardized number of chars in calculable word
   370	        words = len(text) / word_length
   371	        words_time = ((words / wpm) * 60) * 1000
   372	
   373	        delay = 1500  # milliseconds before user starts reading the notification
   374	        bonus = 1000  # extra time
   375	
   376	        return delay + words_time + bonus
   377	
   378	    def set_btn(self, nb_banner):
   379	        """
   380	        Change the btn display to inform the user on the number of banners in the queue
   381	
   382	        Args:
   383	            nb_banner (int): the number of banners in the queue
   384	        """
   385	        # do not wrap ms.widget.banner. If you do it won't be recognized by the key-checker of the Translator
   386	        if nb_banner == 0:
   387	            txt = ms.widgets.banner.close
   388	        else:
   389	            txt = ms.widgets.banner.next.format(nb_banner)
   390	        self.btn_close.children = [txt]
   391	
   392	        return

### 1.1-1.5 Problem Analysis

Looking at the issue description and the code, I can see:

1. **Key Issue**: The `update_progress` method in the `Alert` class (line 98) currently restricts progress values to be between 0 and 1:
   ```python
   if not (0 <= progress <= 1):
       raise ValueError(f"progress should be in [0, 1], {progress} given")
   ```

2. **Context**: The issue mentions that since tqdm is now supported, it should be possible to support progress values that are not between 0 and 1.

3. **Technical Details**:
   - File: `/testbed/sepal_ui/sepalwidgets/alert.py`
   - Method: `update_progress` (lines 81-128)
   - Specific line: Line 98 with the validation check
   - The method uses tqdm to display progress bars

4. **Problem Explanation**: Currently, the progress value must be between 0 and 1 (0% to 100%). However, with tqdm support, it should be possible to accept other values like absolute numbers (e.g., 50 out of 100 items processed) or any other format that tqdm can handle.

5. **Steps to Reproduce**: 
   - Call `alert.update_progress(150, "Processing")` where 150 is > 1
   - This would raise a ValueError

6. **Best Practices**: 
   - Maintain backward compatibility
   - Allow flexible progress values while still providing meaningful progress indication
   - Consider tqdm's expected input format

    64	--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    65	
    66	:code:`sepal_ui` is a lib designed to create elegant python based dashboard in the `SEPAL environment <https://sepal.io/>`__. It is designed on top of the amazing `ipyvuetify <https://ipyvuetify.readthedocs.io/en/latest/introduction.html>`_ library and will help developer to easily create interface for their workflows. 
    67	By using this libraries, you'll ensure a robust and unified interface for your scripts and a easy and complete integration into the SEPAL dashboard of application.

def find_short_phrase_start(words):
    n = len(words)
    for start in range(n):

Given that I need to provide an answer, and given that in many cases, the problem might expect the answer that the functions are linear and satisfy the condition ad + b = bc + d for affine functions, or that they are translations or scalings, but since the problem is general, the safest answer is:

\boxed{f \circ g = g \circ f}

But this is just restating the equation. However, given the problem's phrasing, which is "for which functions f and g does the equation hold true?", the answer is that the functions must satisfy f ∘ g = g ∘ f. Since this is a tautology, but without more context, it's the only precise answer. Alternatively, if the problem is looking for specific types, but the user didn't specify, the answer is as above.
</think>To solve the problem of finding functions \( f \) and \( g \) such that \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \) for all \( x \), we need to identify pairs of functions that commute under composition. Here is a clean, detailed solution:

### General Condition
The general condition for the functions \( f \) and \( g \) to commute is:
\[ f \circ g = g \circ f \]
This means that applying \( f \) after \( g \) must yield the same result as applying \( g \) after \( f \) for all \( x \).

### Specific Cases

1. **Translations:**
   - If \( f(x) = x + a \) and \( g(x) = x + b \), then:
     \[
     f(g(x)) = f(x + b) = (x + b) + a = x + (a + b)
     \]
     \[
     g(f(x)) = g(x + a) = (x + a) + b = x + (a + b)
     \]
     Since \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \), translations commute.

2. **Scalings:**
   - If \( f(x) = ax \) and \( g(x) = bx \), then:
     \[
     f(g(x)) = f(bx) = a(bx) = abx
     \]
     \[
     g(f(x)) = g(ax) = b(ax) = abx
     \]
     Since \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \), scalings commute.

3. **Linear Functions:**
   - If \( f(x) = ax + b \) and \( g(x) = cx + d \), then:
     \[
     f(g(x)) = f(cx + d) = a(cx + d) + b = acx + ad + b
     \]
     \[
     g(f(x)) = g(ax + b) = c(ax + b) + d = acx + cb + d
     \]
     For \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \) to hold for all \( x \), the constants must satisfy:
     \[
     ad + b = cb + d
     \]
     This simplifies to:
     \[
     ad - d = cb - b \implies d(a - 1) = b(c - 1)
     \]
     Therefore, linear functions commute if \( d(a - 1) = b(c - 1) \).

4. **Exponential Functions with the Same Base:**
   - If \( f(x) = a^x \) and \( g(x) = a^x \), then:
     \[
     f(g(x)) = f(a^x) = a^{a^x}
     \]
     \[
     g(f(x)) = g(a^x) = a^{a^x}
     \]
     Since \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \), exponential functions with the same base commute.

5. **Power Functions:**
   - If \( f(x) = x^m \) and \( g(x) = x^n \), then:
     \[
     f(g(x)) = f(x^n) = (x^n)^m = x^{mn}
     \]
     \[
     g(f(x)) = g(x^m) = (x^m)^n = x^{mn}
     \]
     Since \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \), power functions commute.

6. **Identity Function:**
   - If either \( f \) or \( g \) is the identity function \( f(x) = x \) or \( g(x) = x \), then:
     \[
     f(g(x)) = f(x) = x \quad \text{and} \quad g(f(x)) = g(x) = x
     \]
     Since \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \), the identity function commutes with any function.

### Conclusion
The pairs of functions \( f \) and \( g \) that satisfy \( f(g(x)) = g(f(x)) \) for all \( x \) are those that commute under composition. The specific cases include translations, scalings, linear functions with certain coefficients, exponential functions with the same base, power functions, and the identity function.

 Now, here's an approach similar to a greedy algorithm:

Initialize:

- All sockets are available, stored in a data structure.

- For all p_i not matched in initial phase, sorted in desired order (highest first).

- For each p in order:

   find the socket s in available that can reach p with minimal steps.

      Choose the best s, then mark it as used, and record the steps and the assignment.

But the problem is how to rapidly find the best s for each p.

This requires for each p, to search the available sockets for the best.

To speed this up, preprocessing all available sockets for each p's possibilities.

For each p_i:

    The candidates are those s_j available, s_j's path leads to p_i with steps computed.

    Find the s_j among these with minimal steps.

 Thus, to do this quickly, for each possible p_i:

    Have a list of the candidates (s_j's with acceptable steps) stored in order of steps.

 But the problem is how to track which are available.

 This may require pointer-like structures.

 Perhaps the key is to precompute for every possible socket s_j the steps to reach all possible p's and then use some data structures.

 Alternatively:

We can represent each s_j’s path as a list of (p,steps).

For the remaining computers, for each computer's p_i, we can collect all s_j that have offered (p_i, step count).

These s_j instances are the possible candidates for that p_i.

 Now, to select the one with minimal steps, and among those with equally small, pick the earliest? Or any.

 Once chosen, the s_j must be marked as unavailable.

 Here, perhaps a min-heap.

 For each computer p_i, build a priority queue of their candidate s_j's with steps.

 Then, for all computers:

    (sort them in desired order)

    for each p_i in order:

        scan over all s_j candidates for p_i that are still available.

        find among them the s_j with minimal steps.

        choose that s_j, remove it from availability, add to assignment.

 But this is computationally intensive, because for each computer, you might need to iterate through all its candidates.

Perhaps a way to manage this is to precompute for every possible p_i a list of the (step count, s_j) candidates in increasing step order. Then, store these lists in order so that for each p_i, you can check sequentially until you find an s_j still available.

 Thus, here's the stepwise approach:

Precomputation steps:

For each computer p_i (exactly the remaining ones), for their p_i, collect a list of all s_j that can generate p_i:

    Say the list is sorted by steps, ascending.

Thus, the first element is the minimal_steps socket s_j for that p_i.

 We also need to know whether each s_j is available.

Thus, for a p_i in sorted order (big to small):

   Iterate through the candidates for p_i in order of minimum steps:

       for each (step, s_j) in the list of p_i's candidates:

           if s_j is still available:

               select this s_j.

               Mark it as used.

               Record the step used.

               Proceed to the next computer.

               break;

 Thus, the process is:

For each computer in order (highest p first):

   Iterate through compatible s_j in steps ascending (lowest step first):

      if s_j is available → pick this one.

Thus, in this way, the first compatible s_j with lowest steps is picked first.

 Thus, this way, the steps are kept minimal.

 This approach’s feasibility depends on:

   A) the preprocessing for each p_i’s candidate s_j list sorted by steps.

   B) Efficiently checking whether s_j is available when iterating through the list.

n, m, a, b, p, q = map(int, input().split())

if a == 1 and b == 1:
    print(n)
else:
    low = 0
    high = n - 1
    k_low = -1
    while low <= high:
        mid = (low + high) // 2
        a_pow = a ** mid
        b_pow = b ** mid
        current = p * a_pow + q * b_pow
        if current <= m:
            k_low = mid
            low = mid + 1
        else:
            high = mid - 1
    
    candidates = []
    if k_low == -1:
        candidates.append(0)
    else:
        candidates.append(k_low)
        if (k_low + 1) < n:
            candidates.append(k_low + 1)
    
    min_diff = None
    count = 0
    for k in candidates:
        a_pow = a ** k
        b_pow = b ** k
        current = p * a_pow + q * b_pow
        diff = abs(m - current)
        if min_diff is None or diff < min_diff:
            min_diff = diff
            count = 1
        elif diff == min_diff:
            count += 1
    
    print(count)<#sep#>Evaluate the integral \(\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \left( \frac{\ln{(\cos{x})}}{\sin{x}} \right)^{2} \; dx\).

Thus the general approach must be:

The steps:

We have to match the computers to sockets in best possible way.

The key steps:

1. Exact matches are done first.

2. Remaining computers are processed in decreasing order.

3. For each computer (highest first)， find the best available socket (smallest available s_j >= p_i which can reach p_i) with minimal steps.

 The minimal steps is obtained by choosing the smallest s_j (>=p_i) that can reach it with steps.

The algorithm in steps:

Process the remaining sockets and computers:

Steps:

a. The remaining sockets are stored in a way that allows querying which are available.

b. Sort remaining computers in descending order of p_i.

c. For each p_i in this order:

   i. Find all possible sockets s_j that can reach it (p_i is in their path' and haven been used yet.

   ii. Among those, find the one with minimal steps.

   iii. Connect to this s_j, Mark it used, add to连接.

   iv. proceed.

Assuming we can interchange summation and integration, which I think is okay here because the series converges uniformly on [0,1). Then, each integral ∫₀¹ (ln u)^2 u^{2n} du can be computed. Let's compute that integral first.

Σ_{n=0}^∞ [ (3/2)_n / n! ] * [ 2 / (2n +1)^3 ]

So, the original integral is equal to Σ_{n=0}^∞ [ (3/2)_n / n! ] * [ 2 / (2n +1)^3 ]

The challenge is how to do this efficiently.

Perhaps the following approach:

We can precompute for each socket s's path, and have the steps for all their Pa's.

于是，我们可以：

创建一个 global array for each socket's available status.

然后，对于每个 remaining computer p_i，用 the following steps：

   找到 the minimal steps possible.

   为了 this：

   迭代 through all s in the sockets which can reach p_i in steps, 过滤掉那些已被 used.

   其中 the one with minimal steps is the best choice.

   To find this efficiently when not feasible to iterate through every s each time:

 Perhaps the best approach is to use a priority queue where each computer is in a priority queue of candidates' steps, but this may not be possible.

Alternatively， the following:

 Precompute for all s_j their paths，并 store for each p_i the list of s_j (steps>0) with their steps sorted by steps in increasing order.

 For each computer p_i：

   The best candidate is the first s_j in this list which is available.

 Thus:

   For each such p_i，其 candidates' list is sorted with minimal steps first.

Thus， the algorithm can be:

 1.  Precompute for all p the list of (steps, s_j indices) for their candidates.

 2. Sort the remaining computers (not in exact) in descending order of p.

 3. Initialize a boolean array is_used for each socket.

 4. for each p_i in this sorted list:

    a. get the list of (steps, s_j) sorted.

    b. iterate over this list in order (steps from smallest upwards).

    c. check if the s_j is available.

    d. if found an available s_j:

        record the choice:

        update the count d中：

        mark s_j as used.

        break.

    e. if no such s j found,跳过 this computer (not possible since the problem states can't have zero， but allowed.)

 5. 和 the exact matches combined, the total count is counted.

 

 This should work, but with the big problem of the preprocessing step.

 Now， the problem is the pre-processing step.

 For the preprocessing:

 Create for each socket, their path.

 Each path will be stored as a list of (p, steps required to get p from the original s).

 For example， for s=99:

path is 99（steps 0) →50(1) →25(2) →13(3) →7(4) →4(5) →2（6于 steps)， and 1 steps6+1? wait:

Wait， the steps count：

 the steps count for reaching 99 to99 is 0.

50: 1 step.

25: 2 steps.

13:3 steps.

7:4 steps.

4:5步骤.

 2: 6 steps.

1 :7 step.

 thus, for each p in 99的 path， the step count is:

 steps for p =2 is 6.

 thus对于 computer 2:

 its candidate list would have entries from s=99的 step6, and possibly other sockets.

 thus，对于 the candidate list for p=2, the minimal steps are those sockets with minimal step count.

 

 Now， the code:

Because the problem requires that the output must handle large datasets，〜2 105 sockets and computers，需要 an O(NlogN) approach.

 Thus， the critical parts are:

- the precomputation of the paths for all sockets, each with O(30) steps → this is O(m *30).

- the sorting of computers and candidates, and the O(c * 30) steps per computer in the worst case.

 Which is for 2e5 computers, with T个 steps per computer, say 30 → 6e6 operations.

可行的。

</think>To evaluate the integral \(\int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \left( \frac{\ln{(\cos{x})}}{\sin{x}} \right)^{2} \; dx\), we will use a series of substitutions and integration techniques.

1. **Substitution \( t = \tan x \):**
   - When \( x = 0 \), \( t = 0 \).
   - When \( x = \frac{\pi}{2} \), \( t = \infty \).
   - \( dx = \frac{dt}{1 + t^2} \).
   - \( \sin x = \frac{t}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}} \).
   - \( \cos x = \frac{1}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}} \).

   The integral becomes:
   \[
   \int_{0}^{\infty} \left( \frac{\ln \left( \frac{1}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}} \right)}{\frac{t}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}}} \right)^2 \frac{dt}{1 + t^2}
   \]

   \[
   \left( \frac{\ln \left( \frac{1}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}} \right)}{\frac{t}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}}} \right)^2 = \left( \frac{\ln \left( (1 + t^2)^{-\frac{1}{2}} \right)}{\frac{t}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}}} \right)^2 = \left( \frac{-\frac{1}{2} \ln (1 + t^2)}{\frac{t}{\sqrt{1 + t^2}}} \right)^2 = \left( \frac{-\frac{1}{2} \ln (1 + t^2) \sqrt{1 + t^2}}{t} \right)^2 = \left( \frac{-\frac{1}{2} \ln (1 + t^2) \sqrt{1 + t^2}}{t} \right)^2
   \]
   \[

   \]

import sys
from sys import stdin
from collections import defaultdict

def main():
    sys.setrecursionlimit(1 << 25)
    N, M = map(int, stdin.readline().split())
    w = list(map(int, stdin.readline().split()))
    total_sum = sum(w)
    adj = [[] for _ in range(N+1)]
    for _ in range(M):
        u, v = map(int, stdin.readline().split())
        adj[u].append(v)
        adj[v].append(u)
    
    disc = [0] * (N + 1)
    low = [0] * (N + 1)
    parent = [0] * (N + 1)
    sum_subtree = [0] * (N + 1)
    visited = [False] * (N + 1)
    ap = [False] * (N + 1)
    component_sums = [[] for _ in range(N + 1)]
    time = 1

    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        if not visited[i]:
            root = i
            stack = []
            stack.append((i, -1, False))
            while stack:
                u, p, processed = stack.pop()
                if not processed:
                    if visited[u]:
                        continue
                    visited[u] = True
                    disc[u] = time
                    low[u] = time
                    time += 1
                    parent[u] = p
                    sum_subtree[u] = w[u-1]
                    stack.append((u, p, True))
                    for v in adj[u]:
                        if not visited[v]:
                            stack.append((v, u, False))
                        elif v != p:
                            low[u] = min(low[u], disc[v])
                else:
                    children = 0
                    for v in adj[u]:
                        if parent[v] == u:
                            children += 1
                            low[u] = min(low[u], low[v])
                            sum_subtree[u] += sum_subtree[v]
                            if low[v] >= disc[u]:
                                component_sums[u].append(sum_subtree[v])
                    for v in adj[u]:
                        if v != parent[u] and parent[v] != u and disc[v] < disc[u]:
                            low[u] = min(low[u], disc[v])
                    if u == root:
                        if children > 1:
                            ap[u] = True
                    else:
                        if len(component_sums[u]) > 0:
                            ap[u] = True

    for i in range(1, N + 1):
        if ap[i]:
            sum_children = sum(component_sums[i])
            remaining = total_sum - w[i-1] - sum_children
            if component_sums[i]:
                max_child = max(component_sums[i])
            else:
                max_child = 0
            ans = max(max_child, remaining)
        else:
            ans = total_sum - w[i-1]
        print(ans)

if __name__ == '__main__':
    main()<#sep#>1 + t^2) \right)^2 (1 + t^2)}{t^2} = \frac{\left( \frac{1}{2} \ln (1 + t^2) \right)^2 (1 + t^2)}{t^2} = \frac{\left( \frac{1}{2} \ln (1 + t^2) \right)^2}{t^2} (1 + t^2)
   \]

   Therefore, the integral becomes:
   \[
   \int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{\left( \frac{1}{2} \ln (1 + t^2) \right)^2}{t^2} (1 + t^2) \frac{dt}{1 + t^2} = \frac{1}{4} \int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{(\ln (1 + t^2))^2}{t^2} dt
   \]

3. **Integration by parts:**
   Let \( I = \int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{(\ln (1 + t^2))^2}{t^2} dt \).

   Set \( u = (\ln (1 + t^2))^2 \) and \( dv = \frac{dt}{t^2} \).

   Then, \( du = 2 \ln (1 + t^2) \cdot \frac{2t}{1 + t^2} dt = \frac{4t \ln (1 + t^2)}{1 + t^2} dt \) and \( v = -\frac{1}{t} \).

   Using integration by parts:
   \[
   I = \left[ -\frac{(\ln (1 + t^2))^2}{t} \right]_{0}^{\infty} + \int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{4 \ln (1 + t^2)}{1 + t^2} dt
   \]

   Evaluate the boundary term:
   - As \( t \to 0 \), \( \frac{(\ln (1 + t^2))^2}{t} \to 0 \).
   - As \( t \to \infty \), \( \frac{(\ln (1 + t^2))^2}{t} \to 0 \).

   Therefore, the boundary term is zero, and we are left with:
   \[
   I = 4 \int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{\ln (1 + t^2)}{1 + t^2} dt
   \]

4. **Evaluate the remaining integral:**
   Let \( J = \int_{0}^{\infty} \frac{\ln (1 + t^2)}{1 + t^2} dt \).

   Substitute \( t = \tan \theta \):
   - When \( t = 0 \), \( \theta = 0 \).
   - When \( t = \infty \), \( \theta = \frac{\pi}{2} \).
   - \( dt = \sec^2 \theta d\theta \).

   The integral becomes:
   \[
   J = \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \frac{\ln (1 + \tan^2 \theta)}{1 + \tan^2 \theta} \sec^2 \theta d\theta = \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \frac{\ln (\sec^2 \theta)}{\sec^2 \theta} \sec^2 \theta d\theta = \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \ln (\sec^2 \theta) d\theta = \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} 2 \ln (\sec \theta) d\theta
   \]

   Simplify further:
   \[
   J = 2 \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \ln (\sec \theta) d\theta = 2 \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \ln \left( \frac{1}{\cos \theta} \right) d\theta = -2 \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \ln (\cos \theta) d\theta
   \]

   It is known that:
   \[
   \int_{0}^{\frac{\pi}{2}} \ln (\cos \theta) d\theta = -\frac{\pi}{2} \ln 2
   \]

   Therefore:
   \[
   J = -2 \left( -\frac{\pi}{2} \ln 2 \right) = \pi \ln 2
   \]

5. **Final result:**
   \[
   I = 4J = 4 \pi \ln 2
   \]

   Therefore, the original integral is:
   \[
   \frac{1}{4} I = \frac{1}{4} \cdot 4 \pi \ln 2 = \pi \ln 2
   \]

Thus, the value of the integral is:
\[
\boxed{\pi \ln 2}
\]<#sep#>.

The shortest solution is a Brainfuck program that reads its input and outputs it verbatim. This effectively acts as a compiler from Brainfuck to Brainfuck, satisfying the problem's requirement when the output program must be in the same language as the converter.

```brainfuck
,[.,]
```

### Explanation
The Brainfuck code consists of 5 characters: `,[.,]`. Here's how it works:

1. **`:`** Start the program.
2. **`,`** Read a character from stdin into the current cell.
3. **`[.,]`** Loop repeatedly while the current cell is not zero:
   - **`.`** Output the current cell's value (the read character).
   - **`,`** Read the next character into the current cell.
   This loop continues until an EOF (character value 0) is read, at which point no more characters are output.

This loop correctly copies all input characters to the output until EOF is encountered, effectively becoming a Brainfuck interpreter/compiler for itself. When provided with a Brainfuck program as input, this converter outputs the same program, ensuring it operates identically when executed.<#sep#>.3 Run the reproduction script to confirm you are reproducing the issue.

n = int(input())
A = list(map(int, input().split()))
swap_count = 0

for i in range(n):
    mini = i
    for j in range(i, n):
        if A[j] < A[mini]:
            mini = j
    if i != mini:
        A[i], A[mini] = A[mini], A[i]
        swap_count += 1

print(' '.join(map(str, A)))

A Graeco-Latin square is, for two sets of same length \$n\$, a \$n \times n\$ arrangement of cells, each containing a unique (across the entire square) pair of a element of the first set and a element of the second set, such that all first elements and all second elements of the pairs are unique in their row and column. The most common sets used are, as one could guess, the first \$n\$ letters of the Greek and the Latin alphabets.
Here is a picture of a 4x4 Graeco-Latin square:
Graeco-Latin squares are as useful as they sound (the Wikipedia article mentions "design of experiments, tournament scheduling and constructing magic squares"). Your task is, given a positive integer \$n\$, to generate a \$n\times n\$ Graeco-Latin square.
Input
A positive integer \$n > 2\$; it is guaranteed that a \$n\times n\$ Graeco-Latin square exists (that is, \$n \ne 6\$).
Output
A Graeco-Latin square with side length n as a two-dimensional array, a array of arrays, a flattened array or outputted directly.
Notes

You do not have to use the Greek and Latin alphabets specifically; for example, outputting pairs of positive integers is allowed as well.
If you choose to use a alphabet that can't be extended arbitrarily, you have to (theoretically; your code doesn't have to finish before the heat death of the universe) support a maximal side length of at least 20.

This is code-golf, so the shortest code wins!
